> 106	const LAYERS = spec.layers ?? [];
> 107	for (const l of LAYERS) {
> 108	  if (!(l.plane in PLANES)) { console.error(`layer ${l.id ?? "?"} aponta plano inexistente: ${l.plane}`); process.exit(2); }
> 109	}
> 110	const HUD = spec.hud ?? {};
> 111	const ATMO = spec.atmosphere ?? { weave: true, horizon: true, nebulae: [] };
> 112	
> 113	const fontB64 = readFileSync(
> 114	  "/home/gcrUX/htdocs/constellation-orchestra/scripts/hyperframes/templates/fonts/Montserrat-variable.ttf",
> 115	).toString("base64");
> 116	
> 117	const esc = (s) => String(s ?? "")
> 118	  .replace(/&/g, "&amp;").replace(/</g, "&lt;").replace(/>/g, "&gt;")
> 119	  .replace(/"/g, "&quot;").replace(/'/g, "&#39;");
> 120	const srcUrl = (p) => (p && p.startsWith("/") ? `file://${p}` : p ?? "");
> 121	const num = (v, d = 0) => Number.isFinite(Number(v)) ? Number(v) : d;
> 122	
> 123	// ── ANCORAGEM UNIFORME (achado do Diretor de Arte, 2026-08-11): TODA camada e posicionada pelo
> 124	//    seu CENTRO — e o centro e o que as 4 lentes escrevem nas Partituras. Os kinds de largura
> 125	//    intrinseca (o conteudo dita a caixa) nao tem w/h para subtrair, entao sao centrados por
> 126	//    translate(-50%,-50%), que o runtime PREFIXA em todo transform que escrever neles.
> 127	//    Antes desta correcao metade das camadas nascia deslocada de meia-caixa. ────────────────────
> 128	const CENTER_KINDS = new Set(["cutout", "chip", "badge", "counter", "stamp"]);
> 129	
> 130	// ── VOC-ART-M-005 (Diretor de Arte): `style` por camada nos kinds de texto. Destrava o piso
> 131	//    tipografico do DoP, o token da casa "gold so para VALOR" e o contador sem tremor de largura
> 132	//    (numeral tabular). Sem isso, tipo e cor eram constantes do motor — decisao de arte na mao
> 133	//    do engenheiro. ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
> 134	function styleAttr(s) {
> 135	  if (!s) return "";
> 136	  const css = [];
> 137	  if (s.size) css.push(`font-size:${num(s.size, 16)}px`);
> 138	  if (s.color) css.push(`color:${esc(s.color)};-webkit-text-fill-color:${esc(s.color)};background:none`);
> 139	  if (s.weight) css.push(`font-weight:${num(s.weight, 700)}`);
> 140	  if (s.tracking) css.push(`letter-spacing:${s.tracking}`);
> 141	  if (s.numerals === "tabular") css.push("font-variant-numeric:tabular-nums;font-feature-settings:'tnum' 1");
> 142	  return css.length ? ` style="${css.join(";")}"` : "";
> 143	}
> 144	
> 145	// ── HTML por kind. A FOLHA de toda camada leva data-leaf="<i>" — e nela (nunca no .pl)
> 146	//    que o runtime escreve opacity/filter. ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────
> 147	function layerHtml(l, i) {
> 148	  const x = num(l.x), y = num(l.y), w = num(l.w, 300), h = num(l.h, 200);
> 149	  const pos = `left:${x - w / 2}px;top:${y - h / 2}px;width:${w}px;height:${h}px`;
> 150	  switch (l.kind) {
> 151	    case "slab": {
> 152	      const isVideo = l.src?.endsWith(".mp4") || l.src?.endsWith(".webm") || l.media === "video";
> 153	      const media = isVideo
> 154	        ? `<video src="${esc(srcUrl(l.src))}" muted playsinline preload="auto" data-vid="${i}"></video>`
> 155	        : `<img src="${esc(srcUrl(l.src))}" alt="">`;
> 156	      // VOC-ART-M-004: `plain` = lamina NUA (sem sheen/rim/borda/opacity .95). Existe para marca de
> 157	      // terceiro nao vestir o figurino da casa — chrome de UI capturado tem que aparecer INTACTO.
> 158	      const plain = l.plain === true;
> 159	      return `<div class="slab${plain ? " plain" : ""}" data-leaf="${i}" style="${pos}">
> 160	        ${media}${plain ? "" : `<div class="sheen"></div><div class="rim"></div>`}
> 161	        ${l.tag ? `<div class="tag">${esc(l.tag)}</div>` : ""}</div>`;
> 162	    }
> 163	    case "cutout":
> 164	      // `glow:false` desliga o halo ciano — marca de terceiro nunca pode receber pigmento da casa.
> 165	      return `<div class="cutwrap${l.glow === false ? " noglow" : ""}" data-leaf="${i}" style="left:${x}px;top:${y}px">
> 166	        <img src="${esc(srcUrl(l.src))}" alt="" style="width:${w}px;height:auto"></div>`;
> 167	    case "chip":
> 168	      return `<div class="chip" data-leaf="${i}" style="left:${x}px;top:${y}px">
> 169	        ${l.iconPath ? `<div class="mk"><svg viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="#06121f" stroke-width="2.5"><path d="${esc(l.iconPath)}"/></svg></div>` : ""}
> 170	        <div>${l.t1 ? `<div class="t1"${styleAttr(l.style)}>${esc(l.t1)}</div>` : ""}${l.t2 ? `<div class="t2">${esc(l.t2)}</div>` : ""}</div></div>`;
> 171	    case "badge":
> 172	      return `<div class="badge" data-leaf="${i}" style="left:${x}px;top:${y}px">
> 173	        <svg viewBox="0 0 24 24">${l.iconSvg ?? '<rect x="3" y="5" width="18" height="14" rx="2"/><path d="M3 15l5-5 4 4 3-3 6 6"/>'}</svg></div>`;
> 174	    case "data": {
> 175	      const cols = (l.series ?? []).map((s, bi) => `
> 176	        <div class="col" data-bar="${i}:${bi}" style="height:0px;width:${num(l.colWidth, 126)}px">
> 177	          <div class="glow" style="background:${esc(s.glow ?? "#7c48ff")}"></div>
> 178	          <div class="body" style="background:${esc(s.fill ?? "linear-gradient(180deg,#c4b5fd,#6d28d9)")}"></div>
> 179	          <div class="blab"><div class="n" style="color:${esc(s.color ?? "#ccbcff")}">${esc(s.label)}</div><div class="k">${esc(s.name ?? "")}</div></div>
> 180	        </div>`).join("");
> 181	      return `<div class="dataviz" data-leaf="${i}" style="left:${x - w / 2}px;top:${y - h / 2}px;width:${w}px">
> 182	        ${l.title ? `<div class="dttl">${esc(l.title)}</div>` : ""}
> 183	        <div class="plot" style="height:${num(l.plotH, 320)}px;gap:${num(l.gap, 120)}px">${cols}</div>
> 184	        ${l.caption ? `<div class="dcap">${esc(l.caption)}</div>` : ""}</div>`;
> 185	    }
> 186	    case "splitbar":
> 187	      return `<div class="splitbar" data-leaf="${i}" style="left:${x - w / 2}px;top:${y - h / 2}px;width:${w}px">
> 188	        ${l.title ? `<div class="dttl">${esc(l.title)}</div>` : ""}
> 189	        <div class="sbtrack" style="height:${num(l.barH, 84)}px">
> 190	          <div class="sbL" data-sb="${i}:L"><span class="sbv">${esc(l.left?.label ?? "")}</span><span class="sbk">${esc(l.left?.name ?? "")}</span></div>
> 191	          <div class="sbgap" data-sb="${i}:G"></div>
> 192	          <div class="sbR" data-sb="${i}:R"><span class="sbv">${esc(l.right?.label ?? "")}</span><span class="sbk">${esc(l.right?.name ?? "")}</span></div>
> 193	        </div>
> 194	        ${l.caption ? `<div class="dcap">${esc(l.caption)}</div>` : ""}</div>`;
> 195	    case "timeline": {
> 196	      const rows = (l.rows ?? []).map((r, ri) => {
> 197	        const ticks = (r.marks ?? []).map((m, mi) =>
> 198	          `<div class="tlmark" data-tl="${i}:${ri}:${mi}" style="left:${(m * 100).toFixed(2)}%"></div>`).join("");
> 199	        return `<div class="tlrow"><div class="tllab">${esc(r.name ?? "")}</div>
> 200	          <div class="tlbar" style="height:${num(l.barH, 14)}px">${ticks}</div>
> 201	          <div class="tlcount" data-tlc="${i}:${ri}">${esc(r.countLabel ?? "")}</div></div>`;
> 202	      }).join("");
> 203	      return `<div class="timeline" data-leaf="${i}" style="left:${x - w / 2}px;top:${y - h / 2}px;width:${w}px">
> 204	        ${l.title ? `<div class="dttl">${esc(l.title)}</div>` : ""}${rows}
> 205	        ${l.caption ? `<div class="dcap">${esc(l.caption)}</div>` : ""}</div>`;
> 206	    }
> 207	    case "counter":
> 208	      // numeral tabular por default: numero que cresce sem tremer de largura (PV-1 do Dramaturgo).
> 209	      return `<div class="counter" data-leaf="${i}" style="left:${x}px;top:${y}px">
> 210	        <div class="cv" data-cv="${i}"${styleAttr({ numerals: "tabular", ...(l.style ?? {}) })}>&nbsp;</div>
> 211	        ${l.name ? `<div class="ck">${esc(l.name)}</div>` : ""}</div>`;
> 212	    case "stamp":
> 213	      return `<div class="stamp" data-leaf="${i}" style="left:${x}px;top:${y}px">
> 214	        <div class="sv"${styleAttr(l.style)}>${esc(l.text ?? "")}</div>
> 215	        ${l.source ? `<div class="ssrc">${esc(l.source)}</div>` : ""}</div>`;
> 216	    default:
> 217	      console.error(`kind desconhecido: ${l.kind} (layer ${l.id ?? i}) — Pedido de Vocabulario, nao improviso.`);
> 218	      process.exit(2);
> 219	  }
> 220	}
> 221	
> 222	// agrupa camadas por plano — um .pl por plano nomeado, na ordem do spec.planes
> 223	const byPlane = {};
> 224	LAYERS.forEach((l, i) => { (byPlane[l.plane] ??= []).push([l, i]); });
> 225	const planesHtml = Object.keys(PLANES).map((pid) => {
> 226	  const inner = (byPlane[pid] ?? []).map(([l, i]) => layerHtml(l, i)).join("\n");
> 227	  const extra = pid === (ATMO.horizonPlane ?? Object.keys(PLANES)[0]) && ATMO.horizon
> 228	    ? `${(ATMO.nebulae ?? []).map((n) => `<div class="neb" style="left:${n.x}px;top:${n.y}px;width:${n.w}px;height:${n.h}px;background:${esc(n.color)}"></div>`).join("")}<div id="horizon"></div>`
> 229	    : "";
> 230	  return `<div class="pl" data-plane="${esc(pid)}">${extra}${inner}</div>`;
> 231	}).join("\n");
> 232	
> 233	// runtime config — so o que o seek(t) precisa
> 234	const RT = {
> 235	  W, H, D, P: PERSP, CAM, DRIFT_X, DRIFT_Y,
> 236	  planes: PLANES,
> 237	  focus: FOCUS,
> 238	  layers: LAYERS.map((l, i) => ({
> 239	    i, kind: l.kind, plane: l.plane, z: PLANES[l.plane], ctr: CENTER_KINDS.has(l.kind),
> 240	    in: l.in ?? [0.1, 0.9], out: l.out ?? [Math.max(0, D - 0.9), D - 0.2],
> 241	    rotY: l.rotY ?? null, rotZ: num(l.rotZ, 0), approach: num(l.approach, 0),
> 242	    spring: l.spring ?? (l.kind === "badge" || l.kind === "chip" || l.kind === "stamp" || l.kind === "cutout"),
> 243	    slideX: num(l.slideX, 0), slideY: num(l.slideY, l.kind === "chip" ? 34 : 0),
> 244	    bars: l.kind === "data" ? (l.series ?? []).map((s, bi) => ({ bi, v: num(s.value), max: Math.max(...(l.series ?? []).map((q) => num(q.value)), 1), grow: s.grow ?? [num(l.growFrom, 1.0) + bi * 0.2, num(l.growFrom, 1.0) + 1.5 + bi * 0.2], barMax: num(l.barMax, 230) })) : null,
> 245	    split: l.kind === "splitbar" ? { lv: num(l.left?.value, 50), rv: num(l.right?.value, 50), at: l.splitAt ?? [1.0, 2.2] } : null,
> 246	    tl: l.kind === "timeline" ? (l.rows ?? []).map((r, ri) => ({ ri, marks: (r.marks ?? []).length, from: num(r.from, 1.0), step: num(r.step, 0.35) })) : null,
> 247	    cnt: l.kind === "counter" ? { from: num(l.from), to: num(l.to), win: l.countWin ?? [0.6, 2.6], prefix: l.prefix ?? "", suffix: l.suffix ?? "", decimals: num(l.decimals, 0) } : null,
> 248	  })),
> 249	  hud: {
> 250	    hasKick: !!HUD.kick, hasTitle: !!(HUD.title1 || HUD.title2), hasCap: !!HUD.caption, hasEnd: !!HUD.end,
> 251	    outWin: HUD.outWin ?? [Math.min(3.0, D * 0.45), Math.min(3.8, D * 0.55)],
> 252	    endWin: HUD.endWin ?? [D - 2.0, D - 1.1],
> 253	    scrim: HUD.scrim ?? "left",

> 255	  fades: { in: num(spec.fadeInSec, 0.35), out: num(spec.fadeOutSec, 0.45) },

> 258	const scrimCss = RT.hud.scrim === "bottom"
> 259	  ? `left:0;right:0;bottom:0;height:34%;background:linear-gradient(180deg, transparent, rgba(2,3,8,.72) 34%, rgba(2,3,8,.94))`
> 260	  : `left:0;top:0;bottom:0;width:64%;background:linear-gradient(90deg, rgba(3,4,10,.94) 0%, rgba(3,4,10,.80) 34%, rgba(3,4,10,.42) 66%, transparent 100%)`;
> 261	
> 262	const html = `<!doctype html><html lang="pt-BR"><head><meta charset="utf-8">
> 263	<title>montar-v2 ${esc(spec.cena ?? "")}</title>
> 264	<style>
> 265	@font-face{font-family:'Mont';src:url(data:font/ttf;base64,${fontB64}) format('truetype');font-weight:100 900;font-display:block}

> 267	html,body{width:${W}px;height:${H}px;overflow:hidden;background:#04050a}

> 269	#wash{position:absolute;inset:0;
> 270	  background:
> 271	    radial-gradient(110% 80% at 50% 2%, rgba(78,40,168,.34), transparent 60%),
> 272	    radial-gradient(70% 46% at 50% 104%, rgba(0,150,190,.22), transparent 64%),

> 274	    linear-gradient(#05060c,#03030a 58%,#070512)}
> 275	#weave{position:absolute;left:50%;top:50%;width:1680px;height:1000px;margin-left:-840px;margin-top:-500px;
> 276	  background:

> 281	  will-change:transform;display:${ATMO.weave === false ? "none" : "block"}}

> 283	#cam{position:absolute;inset:0;transform-style:preserve-3d;will-change:transform}
> 284	/* .pl: GEOMETRIA pura. will-change:transform e NADA MAIS (armadilha 1 do header). */
> 285	.pl{position:absolute;left:50%;top:50%;transform-style:preserve-3d;will-change:transform}
> 286	/* coordenadas RELATIVAS AO CENTRO do plano (o .pl e 0x0) — nada de % aqui */
> 287	#horizon{position:absolute;left:-1700px;top:${num(ATMO.horizonY, 380)}px;width:3400px;height:5px;border-radius:50%;
> 288	  background:linear-gradient(90deg,transparent,rgba(0,220,255,.6) 22%,rgba(150,90,255,.62) 62%,transparent);
> 289	  filter:blur(12px)}
> 290	.neb{position:absolute;border-radius:50%;filter:blur(70px);opacity:.34}
> 291	.slab{position:absolute;border-radius:32px;overflow:hidden;background:#080b14;
> 292	  border:1px solid rgba(150,120,255,.32);

> 296	.slab .sheen{position:absolute;inset:0;pointer-events:none;
> 297	  background:linear-gradient(102deg, rgba(255,255,255,.20) 0%, rgba(255,255,255,.03) 20%, transparent 44%)}

> 299	  box-shadow:inset 0 0 70px rgba(124,72,255,.34), inset 0 1px 0 rgba(255,255,255,.20)}
> 300	.tag{position:absolute;left:20px;bottom:18px;font-size:13px;letter-spacing:.17em;text-transform:uppercase;
> 301	  color:#cdeeff;opacity:.85;font-weight:600;text-shadow:0 2px 10px #000}
> 302	/* ancoragem por CENTRO nos kinds de largura intrinseca — o mesmo contrato dos kinds com w/h */

> 340	.sbgap{width:0px;background:#04050a}

> 346	.tlbar{position:relative;flex:1;border-radius:9px;background:rgba(140,195,255,.10);

> 354	.counter .ck{font-size:16px;font-weight:700;letter-spacing:.22em;text-transform:uppercase;color:#9fd8ff;margin-top:12px}
> 355	.stamp{position:absolute;text-align:center;padding:26px 44px;border-radius:22px;white-space:nowrap;
> 356	  background:rgba(16,10,34,.86);border:2px solid rgba(178,120,255,.95);

> 392	<script>
> 393	(function(){
> 394	  var C=${JSON.stringify(RT)};
> 395	  var P=C.P, D=C.D;
> 396	  function ease(x){ return x<=0?0:x>=1?1:1-Math.pow(1-x,3); }
> 397	  function smooth(x){ return x<=0?0:x>=1?1:x*x*(3-2*x); }
> 398	  function spring(x){ return x<=0?0:x>=1?1:1-Math.exp(-6*x)*Math.cos(9*x); }
> 399	  function clamp(v,a,b){ return v<a?a:v>b?b:v; }
> 400	  function seg(t,a,b){ return clamp((t-a)/(b-a),0,1); }
> 401	  function win(t,a,b,c,d){ return ease(seg(t,a,b))*(1-smooth(seg(t,c,d))); }
> 402	
> 403	  // A trilha e [t,x,y,z] em toda parte (o parser normaliza o legado [t,z] para x=0,y=0).
> 404	  function camAt(t){
> 405	    var K=C.CAM, L=K[K.length-1];
> 406	    for(var i=0;i<K.length-1;i++){
> 407	      if(t<=K[i+1][0]){
> 408	        var a=K[i], b=K[i+1];
> 409	        var u=smooth((t-a[0])/(b[0]-a[0]));
> 410	        return { x:a[1]+(b[1]-a[1])*u, y:a[2]+(b[2]-a[2])*u, z:a[3]+(b[3]-a[3])*u };
> 411	      }
> 412	    }
> 413	    return { x:L[1], y:L[2], z:L[3] };
> 414	  }
> 415	  function camZ(t){ return camAt(t).z; }
> 416	  // foco viaja entre estacoes [t, planeId] — mesma interpolacao do benchmark
> 417	  function focusZ(t){
> 418	    var S=C.focus.stations;
> 419	    var f=C.planes[S[0][1]];
> 420	    for(var i=1;i<S.length;i++){
> 421	      var a=S[i][0], b=a+0.8;
> 422	      f += (C.planes[S[i][1]]-f)*smooth(seg(t,a,b));
> 423	    }
> 424	    return f;
> 425	  }
> 426	
> 427	  var cam=document.getElementById('cam');
> 428	  var weaveEl=document.getElementById('weave');
> 429	  var planeEls={};
> 430	  [].slice.call(document.querySelectorAll('[data-plane]')).forEach(function(el){ planeEls[el.getAttribute('data-plane')]=el; });
> 431	  var leafEls={};

> 433	  var kickEl=document.getElementById('kick'),ruleEl=document.getElementById('rule');

> 437	
> 438	  var vids=[].slice.call(document.querySelectorAll('video[data-vid]'));
> 439	  function syncVideos(t){
> 440	    var ps=vids.map(function(v){
> 441	      var d=v.duration;
> 442	      if(!isFinite(d)||d<=0) return Promise.resolve();
> 443	      var vt=t%d;
> 444	      if(Math.abs(v.currentTime-vt)<0.02) return Promise.resolve();
> 445	      return new Promise(function(res){
> 446	        var done=false;
> 447	        function ok(){ if(done)return; done=true; v.removeEventListener('seeked',ok); res(); }
> 448	        v.addEventListener('seeked',ok,{once:true});
> 449	        v.currentTime=vt;
> 450	        setTimeout(ok,300);
> 451	      });
> 452	    });
> 453	    return Promise.all(ps);
> 454	  }
> 455	
> 456	  async function seek(t){
> 457	    if(t<0)t=0; if(t>D)t=D;
> 458	    var cam3=camAt(t), cz=cam3.z, fz=focusZ(t);
> 459	    // trilha autorada + respiro senoidal (aditivo). Trilha zerada = comportamento antigo.
> 460	    var dx=cam3.x+Math.sin(t*0.40)*C.DRIFT_X, dy=cam3.y+Math.cos(t*0.31)*C.DRIFT_Y;
> 461	    cam.style.transform='translate3d('+dx.toFixed(2)+'px,'+dy.toFixed(2)+'px,'+cz.toFixed(1)+'px)';
> 462	
> 463	    var zs=[]; for(var pk in C.planes) zs.push(C.planes[pk]);
> 464	    var zBack=Math.min.apply(null,zs);
> 465	    if(weaveEl){
> 466	      var wEff=cz+zBack, wS=P/(P-wEff);
> 467	      weaveEl.style.transform='translate('+(dx*wS).toFixed(2)+'px,'+(dy*wS).toFixed(2)+'px) scale('+wS.toFixed(4)+')';
> 468	    }
> 469	    for(var pk2 in C.planes){
> 470	      var pel=planeEls[pk2];
> 471	      if(pel) pel.style.transform='translate3d(-50%,-50%,'+C.planes[pk2]+'px)';
> 472	    }
> 473	    // FOCO MOTIVADO — a camera entra na conta.
> 474	    // Antes: dof(z)=|z-fz|/k, so a distancia em Z de CENA. cz estava aqui no escopo, tres
> 475	    // linhas acima, e nunca era usado — entao a camera avancava 380px no dolly e a profundidade
> 476	    // de campo nao mudava um pixel. Opticamente impossivel, e o olho percebe sem saber nomear.
> 477	    // Agora a metrica e a distancia OPTICA ate a camera, como numa lente de verdade: o que esta
> 478	    // perto do plano focal fica nitido, e o desfoque cresce mais rapido no que esta perto da
> 479	    // camera do que no que esta longe — que e a assimetria que faz o quadro parecer fotografado.
> 480	    // cz cai para 1 quando a formula degenera (camera no plano de projecao), preservando o
> 481	    // comportamento antigo em vez de estourar em Infinity.
> 482	    function dof(z){
> 483	      if(!C.focus.motivated) return clamp(Math.abs(z-fz)/C.focus.k,0,C.focus.maxBlur);
> 484	      var dz=P-(cz+z), df=P-(cz+fz);
> 485	      if(dz<=1||df<=1) return C.focus.maxBlur;
> 486	      return clamp(C.focus.k*Math.abs(1/df-1/dz)*P,0,C.focus.maxBlur);
> 487	    }
> 488	
> 489	    var gIn=smooth(seg(t,0,C.fades.in)), gOut=smooth(seg(t,D-C.fades.out,D));
> 490	    var G=gIn*(1-gOut);
> 491	
> 492	    for(var li=0;li<C.layers.length;li++){
> 493	      var L=C.layers[li], el=leafEls[String(L.i)];
> 494	      if(!el) continue;
> 495	      var op=win(t,L.in[0],L.in[1],L.out[0],L.out[1])*G;
> 496	      el.style.opacity=String(op);
> 497	      el.style.filter='blur('+dof(L.z).toFixed(2)+'px)';
> 498	      var tr='';
> 499	      if(L.kind==='slab'&&L.rotY){
> 500	        var open=(L.rotY.base||0)+(L.rotY.open||0)*smooth(seg(t,(L.rotY.openWin||[0,D])[0],(L.rotY.openWin||[0,D])[1]))
> 501	          +Math.sin(t*(L.rotY.wobbleFreq||0.5))*(L.rotY.wobble||0);
> 502	        tr='rotateY('+open.toFixed(2)+'deg)';
> 503	        if(L.approach) tr+=' translateZ('+((1-ease(seg(t,L.in[0],L.in[1])))*-L.approach).toFixed(1)+'px)';
> 504	      } else if(L.spring){
> 505	        var sIn=Math.min(spring(seg(t,L.in[0],L.in[1])),1.06);
> 506	        var parts=[];
> 507	        if(L.rotZ) parts.push('rotateZ('+L.rotZ+'deg)');
> 508	        if(L.rotY&&typeof L.rotY==='number') parts.push('rotateY('+L.rotY+'deg)');
> 509	        if(L.slideX) parts.push('translateX('+((1-sIn)*L.slideX).toFixed(1)+'px)');
> 510	        if(L.slideY) parts.push('translateY('+((1-sIn)*L.slideY).toFixed(1)+'px)');
> 511	        if(L.kind==='badge'||L.kind==='stamp') parts.push('scale('+sIn.toFixed(3)+')');
> 512	        tr=parts.join(' ');
> 513	      } else if(L.approach){
> 514	        tr='translateZ('+((1-ease(seg(t,L.in[0],L.in[1])))*-L.approach).toFixed(1)+'px)';
> 515	      }
> 516	      // A centragem PREFIXA todo transform (e e escrita mesmo quando nao ha animacao) — senao o
> 517	      // primeiro transform do runtime apagaria o translate(-50%,-50%) do CSS e a camada saltaria
> 518	      // meia-caixa no meio da cena.
> 519	      if(L.ctr) el.style.transform='translate(-50%,-50%) '+tr;
> 520	      else if(tr) el.style.transform=tr;
> 521	
> 522	      if(L.bars){
> 523	        for(var b=0;b<L.bars.length;b++){
> 524	          var BR=L.bars[b];
> 525	          var bel=el.querySelector('[data-bar="'+L.i+':'+BR.bi+'"]');
> 526	          if(!bel) continue;
> 527	          var grow=Math.min(spring(seg(t,BR.grow[0],BR.grow[1])),1.06);
> 528	          bel.style.height=(BR.barMax*(BR.v/BR.max)*Math.max(grow,0)).toFixed(1)+'px';
> 529	          bel.style.opacity=String(ease(seg(t,BR.grow[0]-0.1,BR.grow[0]+0.6)));
> 530	        }
> 531	      }
> 532	      if(L.split){

> 585	  function guardImages(cb){
> 586	    var imgs=[].slice.call(document.querySelectorAll('img'));
> 587	    var pend=imgs.length, bad=[];
> 588	    if(!pend) return cb(bad);
> 589	    imgs.forEach(function(im){
> 590	      function done(){ if(--pend<=0) cb(bad); }
> 591	      if(im.complete){ if(!(im.naturalWidth>0)) bad.push(im.getAttribute('src')); done(); }
> 592	      else{
> 593	        im.addEventListener('load',function(){ if(!(im.naturalWidth>0)) bad.push(im.getAttribute('src')); done(); },{once:true});
> 594	        im.addEventListener('error',function(){ bad.push(im.getAttribute('src')); done(); },{once:true});
> 595	      }
> 596	    });
> 597	  }
> 598	  function ready(){ window.__ready=true; }
> 599	  guardImages(function(bad){
> 600	    if(bad.length){ window.__fatal='assets nao carregaram: '+bad.join(' , '); return; }
> 601	    if(vids.length){
> 602	      var pend=vids.filter(function(v){ return v.readyState<1; }).length;
> 603	      if(!pend) ready();
> 604	      else {
> 605	        vids.forEach(function(v){ v.addEventListener('loadedmetadata',function(){ if(--pend<=0) ready(); },{once:true}); });
> 606	        setTimeout(ready,4000);
> 607	      }
> 608	    } else ready();
> 609	  });
> 610	})();

I'm spotting some critical gaps between the champion implementation and the generic runner. The champion applies a color grade filter to the MP4 that the generic version skips entirely. It also runs two additional processing gates—perspective correction and depth-of-field—that the generic runner doesn't execute. The champion also hardcodes a specific lens perspective value rather than reading it from the spec, which might be worth investigating to see what the spec actually specifies.

Now I need to examine all seven specs to understand what values they declare and verify what the GRADE_VF filter does.

## 🤖 Claude *(18:20:19)*

<details>
<summary>💭 Thinking Process</summary>

I'm noticing significant differences in how the specs handle HUD elements—the champion spec includes a full set of title cards, kickers, rules, captions, and end cards, but the generic specs either omit the HUD entirely or include only minimal components like a bottom scrim. This suggests the HUD structure isn't consistently defined across the specifications.

Beyond that, there's a critical issue with the splitbar implementation: the generic spec is missing the `value` property on the left and right objects, so it defaults to a 50/50 split regardless of what the labels claim. Even worse, the `splitAt` timing defaults to [1.0, 2.2], which occurs before the layer even appears on screen—meaning the animation completes invisibly and the bar shows up already fully rendered.

The timeline has a similar problem: without the `from` and `step` properties, it defaults to animating marks between t=1.0 and t≈3.1, but the layer doesn't appear until t=24.81. By then all the marks are already at full opacity, so the entire reveal animation—the whole point of the timeline—is lost. The counter in gancho is fine since it uses fixed from/to/countWin values. The `data` kind field isn't used anywhere in the specs, and there are three nebulae which is correct.

Now moving into the blur task.

I'm looking at the focusZ function, which sequentially accumulates focus values across stations—each one pulls the focus toward its target over 0.8 seconds using a smoothstep interpolation. The key thing is that this happens cumulatively in a loop, so at any given time t, the final focus value is the result of applying all stations in sequence with their respective smooth weights.

There's an important edge case though: if a station's transition hasn't fully completed before the next station begins, the focus won't have reached the previous station's plane yet. I'm checking the timing gaps in the actual data—ep07-gancho has all gaps greater than 0.8 seconds except for one transition that's 2.93 seconds, which is fine. But ato2-adsense has a tighter gap at 23.02 to 24 seconds that I need to verify.

Now I'm looking at how depth-of-field is calculated. The blur amount depends on whether focus is motivated—if not, it's a simple distance-based calculation clamped between 0 and a maximum blur value. The default setup uses a single focal plane at the first plane key with a blur falloff constant of 80 and a max blur of 11.

The blur gets applied directly to each layer's filter style unconditionally, even when opacity is zero. For layers that never appear in any focus station, their blur is permanently determined by their distance from the nearest station plane divided by the falloff constant—so they'll always have some blur unless they happen to share a Z-coordinate with a station. Let me work through the specific planes for gancho to see what blur values each layer gets. I'm realizing that during the transitions between stations, the blur function passes through intermediate z-plane values continuously, so I need to check if caneca's plane at -1900 gets hit during any interpolation. Looking at the station sequence, the transition from operador (-2050) to cifra (-820) does pass through -1900, which means at that moment the blur calculation would apply.

Now I need to write a script that numerically evaluates the focus depth and depth-of-field across the layer's visible window, accounting for the opacity function that defines when the layer is actually on-screen. I'll compute the minimum blur over regions where opacity is significant (above 0.5 threshold) and also report separately over the fully visible range. Let me check the remaining specs and the grade configuration first.

</details>

## 🤖 Claude *(18:20:34)*

<details>
<summary>💭 Thinking Process</summary>

I'm noticing that the cutout elements in ato1 declare a height value but no width, yet the motor renders them with a fixed 300px width (defaulting from line 148) and height set to auto, which means the height declarations are effectively ignored. This is the same "adesivo colado" rendering issue that was noted in the gancho spec and apparently fixed there by adding width.

The spec-contract auditor wouldn't flag this as a problem because `h` is defined in the common fields section outside the switch statement, so it appears to be a valid field—but for cutout elements it's silently lost. The same pattern repeats in ato3 with the mixer element.

Now I need to check the remaining three specs: hedge, fecho, and macro.

</details>

## 🤖 Claude *(18:20:41)*

<details>
<summary>💭 Thinking Process</summary>

I found the core issue — `pat` and `aud` are declared as `chip` kind but they're trying to use `series`, which is exactly what the spec-contract was designed to catch. Since chips read from `l.t1` and `l.t2`, both render as empty divs, and the camera focuses on these blank chips at specific timestamps, creating that visual glitch. I should run the spec-contract auditor across all seven specs to confirm this is the systematic problem.

</details>

## 🤖 Claude *(18:20:52)*

<details>
<summary>💭 Thinking Process</summary>

I found the issue — the auditor reported zero dead fields for `ep07-estrutura-hedge` even though `pat` and `aud` chips carry a `series` field that shouldn't belong to them. It passed validation because `camposComuns()` scans the entire file with the switch statement removed, and `l.series` actually appears outside the switch block in the render component where it's used conditionally for the `data` kind.

This means `series` got classified as a common field valid for all kinds, which blinds the gate that's supposed to catch exactly this bug — a chip with `series`. Let me run the spec contract script to verify what fields are being marked as common.

</details>

## 🤖 Claude *(18:21:15)*

<details>
<summary>💭 Thinking Process</summary>

So these common fields like `series`, `rows`, `left`, `right`, `from`, `to`, `countWin`, `barMax`, `splitAt`, `prefix`, `suffix`, `decimals`, and `growFrom` are all being read by the RT compiler outside the switch statement, which means the auditor accepts them on any kind of element. The combination of `chip` + `series` is exactly the documented failure mode, and the gate is allowing it through.

I also notice that `cutout` doesn't list `w` and `h` in its spec even though they're common fields read at line 148—but the cutout only actually uses `w` at line 166, so declaring just `h` gets accepted by the gate and then silently ignored by the engine.

Now I'm building a blur arithmetic script that implements focusZ, dof, and win exactly as the engine does. For each spec and layer, I'll sample time from 0 to D at 1/30s intervals, compute the opacity and blur values, then report the minimum blur across the window where opacity is at least 0.5 (and separately where it's at least 0.9 for "fully present"). I'm also tracking what fraction of on-screen time has blur under 2 pixels.

I'm classifying readability as LEGIVEL, MARGINAL, or NUNCA LEGIVEL based on whether layers ever achieve blur below 2 pixels during their active window and carry legible information. The thresholds are: LEGIVEL if minimum blur is under 0.5 pixels and stays below 2 for at least around 1 second, MARGINAL if it's between 0.5 and 2.0, and NUNCA LEGIVEL if it's 2.0 or higher.

I need to measure the actual duration below 2 pixels while opacity is at least 0.5, since a layer dipping below 2 pixels for just a few frames as focus racks through isn't truly readable. For information-carrying elements, I'm counting chip text, counters, stamps, timelines, and data as legible content, plus cutouts as figures (since the task specifies "texto, numero, figura") and slabs as screenshots.

Writing the script... Looking at the font sizes across different components—stamps at 46px down to 13px, chips ranging from 15.5px to 13px, counters at 92px and 16px, splitbars at 30px and 12.5px, timeline elements from 22px down to 14px—I can see that a 2px blur completely destroys legibility on the smallest text like those 13px labels. I'll use the tiniest typographic element in each category as my test case for whether blur remains readable. Now I'm writing the script.

</details>


## 🤖 Claude *(18:21:15)*

Now the arithmetic. Writing a script that replicates the engine's `focusZ`/`dof`/`win` exactly.

{
  "file_path": "/tmp/claude-1001/-home-gcrUX-htdocs-constellation-orchestra/0f660c85-dc33-4596-b7d2-6af1ded6fee1/scratchpad/pericia/blur-audit.mjs",
  "content": "#!/usr/bin/env node\n/**\n * PERICIA B \u2014 replica BIT A BIT a aritmetica do motor montar-template-v2.mjs:\n *   focusZ(t)  -> :417-425\n *   dof(z)     -> :482-487\n *   win/ease/smooth/seg -> :396-401\n *   G (fade)   -> :489-491   (fadeIn 0.35 / fadeOut 0.45 default, :255)\n *   defaults   -> focus.k=80 focus.maxBlur=11 stations=[[0, primeiro plano]] (:87-97)\n * Para cada camada: menor blur atingido DENTRO da janela em cena, e por quanto tempo fica < 2px.\n */\nimport { readFileSync, readdirSync } from \"node:fs\";\nimport { join, basename } from \"node:path\";\n\nconst DIR = process.argv[2] ?? \"/home/gcrUX/htdocs/constellation-orchestra/scripts/ep07/scenes\";\n\nconst clamp = (v, a, b) => (v < a ? a : v > b ? b : v);\nconst seg = (t, a, b) => clamp((t - a) / (b - a), 0, 1);\nconst ease = (x) => (x <= 0 ? 0 : x >= 1 ? 1 : 1 - Math.pow(1 - x, 3));\nconst smooth = (x) => (x <= 0 ? 0 : x >= 1 ? 1 : x * x * (3 - 2 * x));\nconst win = (t, a, b, c, d) => ease(seg(t, a, b)) * (1 - smooth(seg(t, c, d)));\n\n// kinds que carregam INFORMACAO LEGIVEL, e o menor corpo tipografico de cada um (CSS do motor)\nconst INFO = {\n  chip: { what: \"texto\", minType: 13 },      // .chip .t2 13px  (:317)\n  counter: { what: \"numero\", minType: 16 },  // .counter .ck 16px (:354) / .cv 92px\n  stamp: { what: \"texto\", minType: 13 },     // .stamp .ssrc 13px (:360) / .sv 46px\n  splitbar: { what: \"texto+numero\", minType: 12.5 }, // .sbk 12.5px (:342)\n  timeline: { what: \"texto+numero\", minType: 14 },   // .tllab 14px (:345)\n  data: { what: \"numero\", minType: 15 },     // .blab .k 15px (:333)\n  cutout: { what: \"figura\", minType: null },\n  slab: { what: \"figura\", minType: null },\n  badge: { what: \"icone\", minType: null },\n};\n\nconst rows = [];\nfor (const f of readdirSync(DIR).filter((x) => x.endsWith(\".spec.json\")).sort()) {\n  const spec = JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(DIR, f), \"utf8\"));\n  const name = basename(f, \".spec.json\");\n  const D = Number(spec.duration);\n  const PLANES = spec.planes ?? {};\n  const FOCUS = {\n    stations: spec.focus?.stations ?? [[0, Object.keys(PLANES)[0]]],\n    k: spec.focus?.k ?? 80,\n    maxBlur: spec.focus?.maxBlur ?? 11,\n    motivated: spec.focus?.motivated === true,\n  };\n  const fadeIn = Number.isFinite(spec.fadeInSec) ? spec.fadeInSec : 0.35;\n  const fadeOut = Number.isFinite(spec.fadeOutSec) ? spec.fadeOutSec : 0.45;\n\n  function focusZ(t) {\n    const S = FOCUS.stations;\n    let fz = PLANES[S[0][1]];\n    for (let i = 1; i < S.length; i++) {\n      const a = S[i][0], b = a + 0.8;\n      fz += (PLANES[S[i][1]] - fz) * smooth(seg(t, a, b));\n    }\n    return fz;\n  }\n  const dof = (z, fz) => clamp(Math.abs(z - fz) / FOCUS.k, 0, FOCUS.maxBlur);\n\n  const stationPlanes = new Set(FOCUS.stations.map(([, p]) => p));\n\n  for (const l of spec.layers ?? []) {\n    const z = PLANES[l.plane];\n    const inW = l.in ?? [0.1, 0.9];\n    const outW = l.out ?? [Math.max(0, D - 0.9), D - 0.2];\n    let minB = Infinity, tMin = null, tUnder2 = 0, tUnder05 = 0, onScreen = 0;\n    const dt = 1 / 60;\n    for (let t = 0; t <= D + 1e-9; t += dt) {\n      const G = smooth(seg(t, 0, fadeIn)) * (1 - smooth(seg(t, D - fadeOut, D)));\n      const op = win(t, inW[0], inW[1], outW[0], outW[1]) * G;\n      if (op < 0.5) continue;\n      onScreen += dt;\n      const b = dof(z, focusZ(t));\n      if (b < minB) { minB = b; tMin = t; }\n      if (b < 2) tUnder2 += dt;\n      if (b < 0.5) tUnder05 += dt;\n    }\n    if (!Number.isFinite(minB)) { minB = NaN; }\n    const info = INFO[l.kind] ?? { what: \"?\", minType: null };\n    let verdict;\n    if (minB >= 2) verdict = \"NUNCA LEGIVEL\";\n    else if (minB >= 0.5 || tUnder2 < 1.0) verdict = \"MARGINAL\";\n    else verdict = \"LEGIVEL\";\n    rows.push({\n      cena: name, id: l.id, kind: l.kind, plane: l.plane, z,\n      estacao: stationPlanes.has(l.plane) ? \"sim\" : \"NAO\",\n      minBlur: minB, tMin, onScreen, tUnder2, tUnder05,\n      carrega: info.what, minType: info.minType, verdict,\n      k: FOCUS.k, maxBlur: FOCUS.maxBlur,\n    });\n  }\n}\n\nconst pad = (s, n) => String(s).padEnd(n);\nlet cena = \"\";\nfor (const r of rows) {\n  if (r.cena !== cena) { cena = r.cena; console.log(`\\n\u2550\u2550 ${cena}   (k=${r.k} maxBlur=${r.maxBlur})`);\n    console.log(`   ${pad(\"id\",11)}${pad(\"kind\",10)}${pad(\"plano\",9)}${pad(\"z\",8)}${pad(\"estacao\",8)}${pad(\"minBlur\",9)}${pad(\"t@min\",8)}${pad(\"s<2px\",8)}${pad(\"s<0.5px\",9)}${pad(\"s em cena\",10)}${pad(\"carrega\",13)}veredito`); }\n  console.log(`   ${pad(r.id,11)}${pad(r.kind,10)}${pad(r.plane,9)}${pad(r.z,8)}${pad(r.estacao,8)}${pad(r.minBlur.toFixed(2),9)}${pad(r.tMin==null?\"-\":r.tMin.toFixed(2),8)}${pad(r.tUnder2.toFixed(1),8)}${pad(r.tUnder05.toFixed(1),9)}${pad(r.onScreen.toFixed(1),10)}${pad(r.carrega,13)}${r.verdict}`);\n}\n\nconsole.log(\"\\n\\n=== RESUMO: camadas com INFORMACAO que NUNCA chegam a <2px ===\");\nconst bad = rows.filter((r) => r.minBlur >= 2 && r.carrega !== \"?\" );\nif (!bad.length) console.log(\"(nenhuma)\");\nfor (const r of bad) console.log(`  ${r.cena} \u00b7 ${r.id} (${r.kind}, ${r.carrega}) minBlur=${r.minBlur.toFixed(2)}px durante ${r.onScreen.toFixed(1)}s em cena`);\n\nconsole.log(\"\\n=== MARGINAIS (tocam foco mas < 1s abaixo de 2px) ===\");\nfor (const r of rows.filter((r) => r.verdict === \"MARGINAL\")) console.log(`  ${r.cena} \u00b7 ${r.id} (${r.kind}) minBlur=${r.minBlur.toFixed(2)}px \u00b7 ${r.tUnder2.toFixed(1)}s abaixo de 2px de ${r.onScreen.toFixed(1)}s em cena`);\n"
}

## 🤖 Claude *(18:22:05)*

<details>
<summary>💭 Thinking Process</summary>

So the blur math actually holds up for most scenes, but there's one catastrophic outlier in ep07-ato2-adsense where 9 of 19 layers never drop below 2px blur—specifically the stamp layers carrying the argument text, which are saturated at the maximum blur value for their entire 4.4 second duration. The issue stems from multiple layers sharing the same depth plane, but the focus stations aren't positioned to handle them properly.

The real problem is a mismatch between when layers appear on screen and when the focus actually visits their depth plane. In the champion's spec, each layer has its own plane and its visibility window aligns with a focus station on that plane, but in ato2, eight stamps are distributed across two planes while the focus only visits those planes at 2-3 specific times. I can verify this by checking whether each information-carrying layer has at least one focus station on its plane within its visible window—that's the legibility gate. Since I already have the plane depths, focus stations, and in/out times from the spec, I can compute this purely through arithmetic without any additional rendering.

Now I need to verify the champion's spec passes this same test. I could extract the spec by running the renderer, but that's expensive at 360 frames. Instead, I'll look for an existing spec.json file from a previous champion run, or check the scratchpad for rendered outputs and contact sheets that could visually demonstrate the blur behavior.

</details>

## 🤖 Claude *(18:22:42)*

<details>
<summary>💭 Thinking Process</summary>

The champion design nails contrast perfectly—the background layers stay intentionally soft and blurred while every information-carrying element reaches crisp clarity. But the generic path has a problem with eight text layers that need attention.

So the hypothesis is partially confirmed, but the real story is more nuanced. The generic path loses critical capabilities at the runner level—grade validation, lens choices, HUD authoring—while the specs themselves are missing key features like perspective transforms, proper splitbar values, timeline markers, and cutout dimensions. The blur issue traces back to authoring: multiple layers sharing planes with beats that don't align to focus stations. I should verify these claims with actual rendering evidence to make this concrete.

I'll write a quick script that generates the HTML template, opens it in a browser, seeks to specific timestamps where the blur is most visible, and captures screenshots at reduced scale. This gives me solid proof without expensive rendering—just one Chromium instance with a few JPEG screenshots at 1720x720.

</details>

## 🤖 Claude *(18:24:31)*

<details>
<summary>💭 Thinking Process</summary>

At t=59.1, the Portuguese text "é por isso que ninguém corta" is completely blurred into illegibility at 10px blur while the cutout of the man remains sharp and clear — creating that exact reproved scene effect. But there's a discrepancy: at t=40, the "o estoque não expira" stamp shows 4.74px blur in the DOM reading, though the observation cuts off before I can see what the actual visual shows.

Looking at the frame itself, "o estoque não expira" appears soft and glowy but still readable — at 4.74px blur on 46px text, the large letterforms survive even if the edges are soft. The timeline stays crisp at 0px, and the designer cutout on the right does show the expected blur at 8.42px. So the measurements align with what I'm seeing. The key insight is that blur destroys small type (13px source lines, 12.5px splitbar keys) at much lower thresholds than large type — my 2px classification threshold works for the small text, but the 46px stamp headline doesn't fully die until around 10px. I need to be precise about where these thresholds actually sit.

A cleaner approach than per-type thresholds: the gate should simply require the layer's plane to be a focus station during its on-screen window — meaning minBlur stays below 1px. That's binary, uses only existing data, and matches what the champion naturally does since every info layer reaches 0.00. Using 1.0px (or ~0.5) as the threshold is more defensible than making aesthetic judgments about type sizes.

Now checking the remaining capability differences between the champion runner and the spec path, starting with the grading step. The champion applies a full grade filter with curves, equalization, color balance, unsharp masking, and vignette, while the generic path skips this entirely. That means the seven scenes come out crude — no lifted blacks, no contrast boost, no saturation lift, no sharpening, and critically, no vignette. The grading engine also registers "montarScene" in a pre-graded engines list downstream, which affects how the pipeline processes these scenes.

The champion also runs perspective and depth-of-field gates that the generic path explicitly disables, so nothing validates that the spec actually produces real perspective or distinct blur planes. And while the champion sets a specific perspective value based on a 47-degree diagonal with a long lens, none of the seven specs declare a camera perspective, so they all fall back to defaults.

For the HUD, the champion includes eight elements (kick, title cards, caption, end cards, and scrim), but the generic specs are mostly missing the HUD key entirely—only gancho has a scrim defined. This means all the HUD checks fail and no kicker, title card, caption, or end card gets rendered.

The scrim div itself is always rendered unconditionally, and it defaults to "left" positioning with a dark gradient that covers 64% of the frame's left side, reaching opacity up to 0.95 around t≈0.9. That's what's creating the dark curtain effect I'm seeing in the frames—ato2-t40 shows it clearly on the left, and ato2-t6.4 has a darker left third. The hudOut value controls this opacity based on the outWin timing window, which defaults to [3.0, 3.8] seconds.

At t=6.4, hudOut is 0, so the scrim opacity should be 0 as well since eOn is also 0 at that point. But here's the real issue: for the first 3.8 seconds of every scene, there's a 64%-wide black curtain covering the left side with absolutely nothing rendered on it—no kick, no title, just a blank dark area. That's a genuine defect. Then at the end, the scrim comes back with opacity 0.90 as it approaches the endWin timing.

So every scene has this unexplained dark curtain appearing at both the start and end. I should verify this by checking specific frames—t=1.0 and t=65.8 of ato2 to confirm the behavior.

Looking at the slab and rotY implementation, the champion uses 4 slabs with rotY parameters that control real perspective rotation, but none of the 7 specs I'm examining has a single slab layer, and rotY only appears as a plain number value rather than as a structured parameter object.

For splitbar, the champion defines explicit values and split positions, but the 2 specs using splitbar don't declare either—they fall back to the engine defaults of 50/50 split at positions 1.0 and 2.2. This creates a contradiction where the bar displays 50/50 while the labels claim 45%/55%.

For timeline, the champion specifies `from` and `step` values that control when marks appear, but the ato2 specs omit these entirely, defaulting to `from=1.0` and `step=0.35`. All 7 marks finish drawing by t≈3.2, but the layer doesn't enter until t=24.81, so the choreographed reveal never happens—the timeline is already complete when it becomes visible.

The cutout width is hardcoded to 300px regardless of the intended height for each object, so all six specs render at the same scale even though they should vary. The gancho spec fixed this but the correction wasn't applied to the others, making every element uniform in size.

Looking at the remaining properties: `data` and `bars` aren't used by the champion, `iconPath` is missing from all seven specs so chips are text-only, `tag` appears in the champion but isn't used anywhere, `plain` and `glow:false` are third-party marks that go unused, and `style` on stamps is actually being used for source lines which is correct. Both the champion and specs handle contact sheets and the ocular gate properly.

One thing worth noting — the generic runner actually has `--disable-dev-shm-usage` which the champion lacks, so that's a place where the generic is better. The champion writes `spec.json` to disk and keeps the spec inline as TypeScript, which is the custom part. I should verify that `render-scenes-montar.ts` handles the alignment between duration and narration correctly, and double-check the claims about the hud and assemble-graph's grade logic. Also need to confirm that B.3 defaults to k=80 and maxBlur=11, and what happens when the spec omits focus entirely.

</details>

## 🤖 Claude *(18:25:05)*

<details>
<summary>💭 Thinking Process</summary>

At t=1.0, the scrim is nearly opaque at 0.95 with a width of 1100.8px covering 64% of the viewport—a dark overlay with no animation kick, title, or end card visible behind it. The cyan rule line sits at the top-left with full opacity, appearing as a floating dash over an empty space. By t=65.8, the scrim fades completely to opacity 0, though the thought cuts off before revealing what happens next.

The real issue is that the end element never triggers because `endEl` is null, so `eOn` remains 0 and no end scrim appears. This confirms the artifact only manifests at the start of scenes without a HUD—that 64% black curtain plus orphan cyan rule persists for roughly the first 3.8 seconds of every such scene.

I'm also noticing that generic-runner scenes get graded nowhere because the grading logic checks for `endEl` existence and registers "montarScene" in the pre-graded engines list, so these scenes skip the grading pipeline entirely. Let me pull up the t=1.0 frame to see this visually.

</details>

<summary>💭 Thinking Process</summary>

At t=1.0 there's a black curtain covering the left side with a stray cyan rule floating at the top-left corner with nothing behind it—no kicker or title visible. This orphaned element persists through the first four seconds of the scene. I've now got what I need for sections A through D, and for section B the default values are k=80 and maxBlur=11, which means if focus is omitted the stations default to a grid configuration at -4200 across all seven specs.

Let me verify this numerically: with fz fixed at -4200 and k=80, the blur calculations for each plane clamp to maxBlur 11—selo at 35.6, gente at 20, forma at 30—so everything stays at maximum blur except for any layers directly on the grid itself, which has none.

For section C on the gate design, I'm looking at where to implement the legibility check most efficiently. The arithmetic validation should live in a dedicated module that can run before the browser even opens, either as part of the spec auditor or right after parsing the JSON in the render script. I'm leaning toward creating a new module specifically for this legibility audit that exports the validation function.

The spec already contains all the data I need—plane IDs with z-values, focus stations, k and maxBlur settings, per-layer plane assignments, and in/out ranges. The missing piece is distinguishing which layers carry information versus which are purely decorative. I could derive this from the layer kind (treating chips, counters, stamps, splitbars, and timelines as information-bearing while cutouts and slabs are figures), but that risks false positives with intentional background elements. The cleaner approach is adding an optional role field to each layer for explicit opt-out control.

For the minimal diff in v1, I'll gate only text-based kinds—there's no legitimate reason to render unreadable text on screen. Figure kinds like slabs and cutouts stay ungated unless explicitly marked as backdrops, which keeps the champion passing without any new field declarations.

The threshold I'm using is a minimum blur of less than 1.0px measured over regions where opacity is at least 0.5. This comes from actual measurements: the smallest glyphs the engine renders are around 12–13px, and a 1px Gaussian blur already destroys the stroke definition at that size. The champion's info layers all hit 0.00px, so this threshold catches the real problems without false positives.

I'm also adding a dwell requirement—the blur must stay below 1.0px for at least 0.6 seconds (18 frames at 30fps) while opacity stays above 0.5. This prevents focus racks that just briefly pass through the threshold from slipping through. For false positives, I'm filtering by text layers only in v1, and atmospheric elements like weave or nebulae don't have blur applied anyway since they're not text layers.

The gate should output the exact fix needed—the `focus.stations` entry to add with the timestamp and plane name. I'm also prioritizing the cheapest corrections: adding a video filter flag to the generic runner is the quickest win (5 minutes, one line in the render script), and it's critical because without it the seven failing scenes ship ungraded and downstream passes can't fix it. Next is removing an orphan scrim rule.

The real leverage is a 2-line engine fix that prevents rendering empty HUD elements across all seven scenes and future specs. Then there's the splitbar issue where the value and split position don't match the label—a truth defect that needs 4 lines across 2 specs. Finally, the hedge chips need their series field renamed to track the two time periods correctly. The real missing pieces are the HUD block and the grade system — those are what actually separate the champion from the specs. The scrim fades over the outWin range [3.0, 3.8] with the default engine value, and the editor's in-window entrance spans [1.36, 2.26], meaning it enters entirely under the curtain — that's a real loss for the character's entrance. I have enough measured evidence to state this confidently, though I'm tempted to grab one more frame at t=2.2 to make it airtight since it's just one page load and it's the entrance of a character. So the editor layer at x=-1180 in plane space actually maps to around 467px in screen space after applying the perspective scale, which falls within the scrim's 1100px coverage—this checks out against the observed frame at t=6.4 where the editor sits near x=410px. O gr\u00e1fico contradiz o pr\u00f3prio n\u00famero que \u00e9 a tese do epis\u00f3dio. E a anima\u00e7\u00e3o de abertura roda em t=1,0\u20132,2s, ANTES de a camada existir (in `[5.65, 6.55]`) \u2014 a revela\u00e7\u00e3o nunca acontece em quadro.",
    "**6. `timeline` sem `from`/`step` \u2192 coreografia morta.** Campe\u00e3o: `pilot-adsense-montar.ts:93-94` autora `from: 8.0, step: 0.22` e `from: 9.3, step: 0.3` por linha. `grep '\"step\"' scripts/ep07/scenes/*.spec.json` \u2192 nenhum. Default `montar-template-v2.mjs:246`: `from=1.0, step=0.35`. As 7 marcas + os 2 contadores da `tl` da ato2 terminam de aparecer em t\u22483,2s, mas a camada s\u00f3 entra em t=24,81s. Frame aberto em t=40s: a timeline aparece J\u00c1 DESENHADA por inteiro. A marca-por-beat \u2014 o \u00fanico motivo de existir do kind \u2014 nunca \u00e9 vista.",
    "**7. `cutout` com `h` em vez de `w` \u2192 escala uniforme silenciosa.** `montar-template-v2.mjs:148` l\u00ea `w = num(l.w, 300)` e `:166` renderiza `width:${w}px;height:auto` \u2014 **`h` nunca \u00e9 lido no cutout**. Auditoria: `ep07-ato1` (caneca h=330, mic h=460, kit h=300), `ep07-ato3` (mixer h=420), `ep07-estrutura-hedge` (folha h=520), `ep07-fecho` (massa h=560), `ep07-macro` (gente h=540) \u2014 **6 camadas em 5 specs, todas sem `w`, todas renderizando 300px**. A corre\u00e7\u00e3o existe e est\u00e1 documentada, mas s\u00f3 na gancho (`ep07-gancho.spec.json:3` _nota: 'o cutout renderiza width:${w};height:auto (:166) e w ausente cai no default 300px') \u2014 nunca foi propagada \u00e0s outras 5.",
    "**8. Os gates G1 (perspectiva) e Gd (dof \u22653 blurs distintos) n\u00e3o rodam.** Campe\u00e3o: `pilot-adsense-montar.ts:127-144` (G1) e `:146-158` (Gd). Gen\u00e9rico: `render-scenes-montar.ts:11` declara por escrito *'(G1 perspectiva/Gd dof rodam no PILOTO \u2014 pilot-adsense-montar.ts \u2014 que valida o motor.)'*. O gen\u00e9rico s\u00f3 mede `bit_rate` e `framemd5` \u2014 e o pr\u00f3prio `spec-contract.mjs:14-16` j\u00e1 sabe que esses dois ficam VERDES num quadro vazio.",
    "**9. `chip` sem `iconPath` \u2192 chip sem marca gr\u00e1fica.** Campe\u00e3o: `pilot-adsense-montar.ts:79,82` d\u00e1 `iconPath` aos dois chips, o que acende o bloco `.mk` (gradiente ciano\u2192violeta, `montar-template-v2.mjs:169,314-315`). `grep iconPath scripts/ep07/scenes/*.spec.json` \u2192 nenhum. Todos os chips do EP07 saem como p\u00edlula de texto puro.",
    "**10. (contra-m\u00e3o, para honestidade) O gen\u00e9rico tem UMA capacidade que o campe\u00e3o n\u00e3o tem:** `render-scenes-montar.ts:45-48` passa `--no-sandbox --disable-dev-shm-usage`, com o achado documentado em `:40-44` (sem isso o chromium morre sempre no frame 566). O campe\u00e3o (`pilot-adsense-montar.ts:118`) n\u00e3o passa nenhum arg. **Os dois runners divergiram e cada um ficou com metade da verdade** \u2014 n\u00e3o existe hoje um caminho que tenha grade E sobreviva a cena longa."

  "blur_mecanismo": "**1. As f\u00f3rmulas, verbatim do motor.**\n\n`focusZ(t)` \u2014 `scripts/motion/montar-template-v2.mjs:417-425`:\n```js\nfunction focusZ(t){\n  var S=C.focus.stations;\n  var f=C.planes[S[0][1]];\n  for(var i=1;i<S.length;i++){\n    var a=S[i][0], b=a+0.8;\n    f += (C.planes[S[i][1]]-f)*smooth(seg(t,a,b));\n  }\n  return f;\n}\n```\n`dof(z)` \u2014 `:482-487` (dentro de `seek`, portanto `fz` e `cz` do instante):\n```js\nfunction dof(z){\n  if(!C.focus.motivated) return clamp(Math.abs(z-fz)/C.focus.k,0,C.focus.maxBlur);\n  var dz=P-(cz+z), df=P-(cz+fz);\n  if(dz<=1||df<=1) return C.focus.maxBlur;\n  return clamp(C.focus.k*Math.abs(1/df-1/dz)*P,0,C.focus.maxBlur);\n}\n```\nAplica\u00e7\u00e3o: `:497` \u2014 `el.style.filter='blur('+dof(L.z).toFixed(2)+'px)'`, **na folha de TODA camada, em TODO frame, incondicionalmente** (inclusive com opacidade 0). Nenhuma das 7 specs declara `focus.motivated`, ent\u00e3o todas usam o ramo linear `|z \u2212 fz| / k`.\n\n**2. O que acontece com um plano que NUNCA \u00e9 esta\u00e7\u00e3o de foco \u2014 a prova.**\n\n`focusZ` s\u00f3 assume valores no INTERVALO entre z's de esta\u00e7\u00f5es consecutivas: cada itera\u00e7\u00e3o puxa `f` para `C.planes[S[i][1]]` e, cumprido o 0,8s, `f` **\u00e9 exatamente** o z daquela esta\u00e7\u00e3o. Portanto o conjunto-imagem de `focusZ` \u00e9 a uni\u00e3o dos segmentos `[z_i, z_{i+1}]` na ordem das esta\u00e7\u00f5es. Consequ\u00eancias:\n\n- se o z do plano da camada **est\u00e1 fora** de toda essa uni\u00e3o \u2192 `dof` tem um piso positivo permanente: `min_t blur = min_i |z_layer \u2212 z_esta\u00e7\u00e3o_i| / k`. A camada **nunca** fica n\u00edtida, em nenhum frame da cena;\n- se o z **est\u00e1 dentro** de um segmento (mesmo sem ser esta\u00e7\u00e3o), a camada atinge 0px **de passagem** \u2014 durante a rampa de 0,8s em que o foco atravessa aquele Z. \u00c9 nitidez de tr\u00e2nsito, n\u00e3o de perman\u00eancia: mede-se em fra\u00e7\u00f5es de segundo. Medido: `ep07-ato1 \u00b7 kit` (plano -1950, n\u00e3o-esta\u00e7\u00e3o, entre `mic` -2150 e `faixa` -1500) chega a **0,02px** em t=25,02 e fica **0,4s** abaixo de 2px em **43,7s** de cena. `ep07-gancho \u00b7 caneca` (-1900, entre `operador` -2050 e `cifra` -820) chega a **1,58px** e nunca abaixo de 0,5px.\n\n**3. Defaults, e o caso de omitir `focus` inteiro.**\n\n`montar-template-v2.mjs:87-97`:\n- `k = spec.focus?.k ?? 80`\n- `maxBlur = spec.focus?.maxBlur ?? 11`\n- `stations = spec.focus?.stations ?? [[0, Object.keys(PLANES)[0]]]` \u2014 **a PRIMEIRA chave de `spec.planes`, na ordem de declara\u00e7\u00e3o do JSON**.\n\nSe o spec omitir `focus` inteiro, `focusZ` vira uma constante = z do primeiro plano. Nas 7 specs do EP07 o primeiro plano declarado \u00e9 sempre `grid` (z = \u22124200), que **n\u00e3o hospeda nenhuma camada** (\u00e9 o plano da atmosfera, `horizonPlane`). Aritm\u00e9tica do desastre, com k=80 e maxBlur=11 sobre a ato2: `gente` \u22122600 \u2192 1600/80 = 20 \u2192 teto **11px**; `forma` \u22121800 \u2192 30 \u2192 **11px**; `selo` \u22121350 \u2192 35,6 \u2192 **11px**; `marca` \u22121000 \u2192 40 \u2192 **11px**. **A cena inteira renderiza no blur m\u00e1ximo, do frame 1 ao \u00faltimo**, e os gates `Gb`/`Gf` do runner gen\u00e9rico passam verdes: um borr\u00e3o em movimento tem bitrate alto e 100% de frames \u00fanicos.\n\nNota adicional: `focus.motivated:true` sem `focus.k` expl\u00edcito \u00e9 falha dura (`:98-101`) \u2014 essa armadilha o motor j\u00e1 protege. A do `focus` ausente, n\u00e3o.",
  "camadas_ilegiveis_por_cena": "Script da aritm\u00e9tica: `/tmp/claude-1001/-home-gcrUX-htdocs-constellation-orchestra/0f660c85-dc33-4596-b7d2-6af1ded6fee1/scratchpad/pericia/blur-audit.mjs` \u2014 replica `focusZ`/`dof`/`win`/`G` linha a linha do motor, amostra a 1/60s e mede o menor blur atingido enquanto `opacity \u2265 0,5`. Sonda de runtime que confirma a conta contra o motor de verdade: `.../pericia/probe-frames.mjs`.\n\n**Classifica\u00e7\u00e3o:** NUNCA LEG\u00cdVEL = minBlur \u2265 2px \u00b7 MARGINAL = minBlur < 2px mas menos de 1,0s abaixo de 2px \u00b7 LEG\u00cdVEL = o resto.\n\n**\u26a0\ufe0f A HIP\u00d3TESE \u00c9 REFUTADA EM 6 DAS 7 CENAS. O desastre de blur \u00e9 UMA cena s\u00f3 \u2014 e \u00e9 total.**\n\n**ep07-ato2-adsense \u2014 9 de 19 camadas NUNCA LEG\u00cdVEIS (8 portam TEXTO):**\n| camada | kind | plano/z | minBlur | tempo em cena |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| s_corta \"\u00e9 por isso que ningu\u00e9m corta\" | stamp | marca \u22121000 | **10,00px (SATURADO no teto)** | 4,4s |\n| s_vende \"o YouTube vende por voc\u00ea\" | stamp | selo \u22121350 | 4,74px | 4,9s |\n| s_prod \"a propor\u00e7\u00e3o desenha o produto\" | stamp | selo \u22121350 | 4,74px | 4,9s |\n| s_estoque \"o estoque n\u00e3o expira\" | stamp | selo \u22121350 | 4,74px | 4,9s |\n| s_preco \"n\u00e3o briga por pre\u00e7o\" | stamp | selo \u22121350 | 3,99px | 4,4s |\n| s_fim \"o formato longo \u00e9 o produto\" | stamp | marca \u22121000 | 3,73px | 0,4s |\n| s_trab \"o trabalho acontece uma vez\" | stamp | marca \u22121000 | 3,68px | 4,4s |\n| s_venda \"a venda se repete\" | stamp | marca \u22121000 | 3,68px | 4,9s |\n| designer (figura humana) | cutout | gente \u22122600 | 8,42px | 9,4s |\n+ MARGINAL: `s_qtd` \"briga por quantidade\" \u2014 0,05px de m\u00ednimo, mas s\u00f3 **0,5s** abaixo de 2px em 4,9s de cena (o foco s\u00f3 ATRAVESSA o plano).\n\n**A causa exata:** 8 stamps compartilham 2 planos (`selo` \u22121350 e `marca` \u22121000), mas o `focus.stations` s\u00f3 visita `selo` UMA vez (t=46,48) e `marca` em t=23,02 e 65,76. Os outros 6 stamps nascem, dizem a frase e morrem enquanto o foco est\u00e1 em `forma` (\u22121800) ou `gente` (\u22122600). |\u22121350 \u2212 (\u22121800)|/95 = **4,74px** \u2014 o n\u00famero bate exatamente com o medido no DOM.\n\n**PROVA DE RUNTIME (lida do `el.style.filter` do motor, n\u00e3o da minha conta):**\n```\n--- ato2 t=6.4    leaf 1 blur=4.83px op=1.00 [stamp] \"o YouTube vende por voc\u00ea\"\n--- ato2 t=40     leaf 9 blur=4.74px op=1.00 [stamp] \"o estoque n\u00e3o expira\"\n--- ato2 t=59.1   leaf 17 blur=10px  op=0.49 [stamp] \"\u00e9 por isso que ningu\u00e9m corta\"\n                  leaf 16 blur=0px   op=1.00 [cutwrap]   \u2190 o boneco de banco de imagem em foco cravado\n```\n**PROVA OCULAR (frame que eu abri):** `.../pericia/frames/ato2-t59_1.jpg` \u2014 a frase inteira \"\u00e9 por isso que ningu\u00e9m corta\" \u00e9 uma MANCHA ileg\u00edvel ocupando o centro do quadro, enquanto o cutout do homem ao lado est\u00e1 cravado em foco. A cena entrega uma foto de banco n\u00edtida e o argumento apagado.\n\n**ep07-ato1-patrocinio \u2014 0 nunca-leg\u00edveis.** 1 MARGINAL: `kit` (cutout, plano `kit` \u22121950 n\u00e3o \u00e9 esta\u00e7\u00e3o) \u2014 0,02px de m\u00ednimo, **0,4s** abaixo de 2px em 43,7s.\n**ep07-ato3-audio \u2014 0 nunca-leg\u00edveis, 0 marginais.** `cpm` (plano \u22121400, n\u00e3o-esta\u00e7\u00e3o) se salva por sorte geom\u00e9trica: fica entre `dl` \u22121450 e `res` \u22121100, e o foco o atravessa por 4,8s.\n**ep07-estrutura-hedge \u2014 0 nunca-leg\u00edveis por blur.** Mas ver o achado abaixo: as 2 camadas de informa\u00e7\u00e3o da cena est\u00e3o **VAZIAS**.\n**ep07-fecho \u2014 0 nunca-leg\u00edveis, 0 marginais.** Todas as 5 camadas chegam a 0,00px.\n**ep07-gancho \u2014 0 nunca-leg\u00edveis.** 1 MARGINAL: `caneca` (plano `caneca` \u22121900, n\u00e3o-esta\u00e7\u00e3o) \u2014 minBlur 1,58px, nunca abaixo de 0,5px.\n**ep07-macro \u2014 0 nunca-leg\u00edveis, 0 marginais.** Todas as 8 chegam a 0,00px.\n\n---\n\n**ACHADO QUE VALE MAIS QUE O BLUR \u2014 `ep07-estrutura-hedge` tem 2 camadas VAZIAS, e o foco cai nelas.**\n\n`ep07-estrutura-hedge.spec.json:148-153` e `:169-174` declaram `pat` e `aud` como `kind:\"chip\"` mas com o campo **`series`**, que pertence ao kind `data`. O motor l\u00ea `l.t1`/`l.t2` no chip (`montar-template-v2.mjs:170`). Resultado medido no DOM:\n```\n--- hedge t=28.5   leaf 2 blur=1.58px op=1.00 [chip] \"\"     \u2190 textContent VAZIO\n                   leaf 3 blur=0px    op=1.00 [chip] \"\"     \u2190 textContent VAZIO, em foco PERFEITO\n```\nFrame aberto (`.../pericia/frames/hedge-t28_5.jpg`): **duas p\u00edlulas roxas vazias** e um borr\u00e3o da multid\u00e3o (10px, e a 300px porque o spec deu `h:520` sem `w`). O quadro est\u00e1 literalmente vazio, com a esta\u00e7\u00e3o de foco apontando para o nada.\n\n**E o gate que existe exatamente para isso passou VERDE.** `node scripts/motion/spec-contract.mjs scripts/ep07/scenes/ep07-estrutura-hedge.spec.json` \u2192 `\u2713 pat chip \u00b7 \u2713 aud chip \u00b7 0 campo(s) morto(s)`. O motivo \u00e9 um bug do auditor: `spec-contract.mjs:60-65` (`camposComuns`) varre o arquivo inteiro com os blocos `case` removidos, e `l.series` aparece no compilador de runtime FORA do switch (`montar-template-v2.mjs:244`). Ent\u00e3o `series` entrou na lista de campos comuns \u2014 v\u00e1lido para QUALQUER kind. Sa\u00edda literal de `node scripts/motion/spec-contract.mjs`: `Campos comuns: approach \u00b7 barMax \u00b7 countWin \u00b7 decimals \u00b7 from \u00b7 growFrom \u00b7 h \u00b7 id \u00b7 in \u00b7 kind \u00b7 left \u00b7 out \u00b7 plane \u00b7 prefix \u00b7 right \u00b7 rotY \u00b7 rotZ \u00b7 rows \u00b7 **series** \u00b7 slideX \u00b7 slideY \u00b7 splitAt \u00b7 spring \u00b7 suffix \u00b7 to \u00b7 w \u00b7 x \u00b7 y`. \u00c9 o defeito n\u00ba 1 do pr\u00f3prio docstring do arquivo (`spec-contract.mjs:7-8`: *'escrevi `chip` com `series` + `colWidth`'*) reincidindo, imune ao anticorpo escrito para mat\u00e1-lo.",
  "gate_legibilidade": "**Onde encaixa:** ANTES do browser abrir. \u00c9 aritm\u00e9tica pura sobre o JSON \u2014 custo zero, sem frame, sem chromium. Dois pontos de entrada, um m\u00f3dulo s\u00f3:\n\n1. `scripts/motion/focus-legibility.mjs` (novo, ~45 linhas) \u2014 `auditLegibility(spec)` devolve `{failures[], warnings[]}`.\n2. `scripts/ep07/render-scenes-montar.ts` \u2014 chamada logo ap\u00f3s o parse do spec (`:55`) e a checagem de `duration` (`:57`), **antes** de `canvasFor` (`:60`) e do `montar-template-v2.mjs` (`:70`). Falha entra em `failures[]` e a cena \u00e9 pulada, exatamente como o G5 FATAL de `:77`.\n3. B\u00f4nus de 1 linha: chamar o mesmo m\u00f3dulo em `scripts/motion/spec-contract.mjs:105`, para que `node spec-contract.mjs <spec>` tamb\u00e9m pegue.\n\n**Que dado J\u00c1 existe no spec:** tudo. `planes{id:z}`, `focus.stations[[t,planeId]]`, `focus.k`, `focus.maxBlur`, e por camada `plane`, `kind`, `in[]`, `out[]`. **Zero declara\u00e7\u00e3o nova \u00e9 necess\u00e1ria para a v1.**\n\n**O que falta declarar (s\u00f3 na v1.1):** um opt-out para figura decorativa. Proponho `\"role\": \"backdrop\"` por camada. Ele \u00e9 necess\u00e1rio porque o campe\u00e3o tem 2 lajes de parede propositalmente saturadas no teto (`far1`/`far2`, minBlur 11,00px medido) \u2014 sem o opt-out o gate reprovaria a pe\u00e7a aprovada, e gate que grita errado \u00e9 pior que gate nenhum (`spec-contract.mjs:54-55`).\n\n**Limiar defens\u00e1vel \u2014 calibrado por MEDI\u00c7\u00c3O, n\u00e3o por gosto:**\n- **`minBlur < 1,0px`**. Justificativa dupla: (a) o menor corpo tipogr\u00e1fico que o motor desenha \u00e9 12,5px (`.sbk`, `montar-template-v2.mjs:342`) e 13px (`.chip .t2` `:317`, `.stamp .ssrc` `:360`) \u2014 um raio de 1px j\u00e1 come o tra\u00e7o dessas letras; (b) **as 5 camadas de informa\u00e7\u00e3o do campe\u00e3o atingem 0,00px medidos** (chip1, chip2, split, tl, cut). O limiar \u00e9 o que a pe\u00e7a aprovada FAZ, n\u00e3o o que eu acho bonito.\n- **`dwell \u2265 0,6s`** (18 frames a 30fps) abaixo de 1,0px enquanto `opacity \u2265 0,5`. Sem o dwell, `s_qtd` passaria por tecnicalidade: minBlur 0,05px porque o foco meramente ATRAVESSA seu plano, com s\u00f3 0,5s de nitidez em 4,9s de cena.\n\n**Como n\u00e3o dispara falso-positivo:**\n- **Fundo/n\u00e9voa/textura n\u00e3o s\u00e3o camadas.** `atmosphere` (weave, horizon, nebulae) \u00e9 renderizada fora do la\u00e7o de camadas (`montar-template-v2.mjs:227-229`), n\u00e3o tem `data-leaf` e nunca recebe `filter`. Fica imune por constru\u00e7\u00e3o.\n- **v1 gateia S\u00d3 os kinds de texto** \u2014 `chip`, `counter`, `stamp`, `splitbar`, `timeline`, `data`. N\u00e3o existe motivo leg\u00edtimo para p\u00f4r palavra ileg\u00edvel em quadro: se o objetivo \u00e9 textura, n\u00e3o se usa texto. Isso captura 8 dos 9 defeitos achados, com **zero** campo novo e **zero** risco de falso-positivo em fundo (fundo nunca \u00e9 `stamp`).\n- **v1.1 estende a `slab`/`cutout`** com o opt-out `role:\"backdrop\"`. Pega o `designer` (8,42px) e mant\u00e9m o campe\u00e3o verde.\n- **Camada que nunca aparece** (`opacity` nunca \u2265 0,5) \u00e9 ignorada, n\u00e3o reprovada.\n\n**Pseudo-diff:**\n\n```diff\n+++ scripts/motion/focus-legibility.mjs           (NOVO)\n+// Gate G-LEGIBILIDADE. Aritmetica pura sobre o spec \u2014 zero render.\n+// Replica focusZ (montar-template-v2.mjs:417-425), dof (:482-487) e win (:401).\n+const TEXT_KINDS = new Set([\"chip\",\"counter\",\"stamp\",\"splitbar\",\"timeline\",\"data\"]);\n+const BLUR_MAX = 1.0;      // menor corpo do motor e 12,5px (.sbk :342); 1px come o traco\n+const DWELL_MIN = 0.6;     // 18 frames @30fps \u2014 barra o foco que so ATRAVESSA o plano\n+\n+export function auditLegibility(spec) {\n+  const P = spec.planes ?? {}, D = Number(spec.duration);\n+  const F = { stations: spec.focus?.stations ?? [[0, Object.keys(P)[0]]],\n+              k: spec.focus?.k ?? 80, maxBlur: spec.focus?.maxBlur ?? 11 };\n+  if (F.motivated) return [];            // metrica optica: fora do escopo da v1\n+  const focusZ = (t) => { let f = P[F.stations[0][1]];\n+    for (let i=1;i<F.stations.length;i++){ const a=F.stations[i][0];\n+      f += (P[F.stations[i][1]]-f)*smooth(seg(t,a,a+0.8)); } return f; };\n+  const out = [];\n+  for (const l of spec.layers ?? []) {\n+    if (!TEXT_KINDS.has(l.kind) && l.role !== undefined) continue;   // v1.1: role:\"backdrop\"\n+    if (!TEXT_KINDS.has(l.kind)) continue;                            // v1: so texto\n+    const z = P[l.plane];\n+    const inW = l.in ?? [0.1,0.9], outW = l.out ?? O que explica as tr\u00eas \u00e9 estrutural e \u00e9 exatamente o que a hip\u00f3tese diz: **o campe\u00e3o \u00e9 um arquivo TypeScript onde 9 capacidades do motor foram escritas \u00e0 m\u00e3o; o caminho por spec exp\u00f5e todas as 9, mas nenhuma delas tem default seguro \u2014 cada campo ausente degrada em sil\u00eancio para um valor que renderiza sem erro.** O Sovereign acerta uma e erra tr\u00eas por constru\u00e7\u00e3o: o campe\u00e3o n\u00e3o \u00e9 melhor de sorte, \u00e9 o \u00fanico artefato onde algu\u00e9m preencheu as 9.\n\n**PARA POSTAR HOJE \u2014 8 edi\u00e7\u00f5es, ~35 linhas, 8 arquivos. Ordenadas por impacto/esfor\u00e7o:**\n\n**BLOQUEADORES DE VERDADE (mentira em quadro \u2014 n\u00e3o d\u00e1 para postar assim):**\n\n1. **`splitbar`: barra 50/50 sob r\u00f3tulo 45%/55%.** [4 linhas \u00b7 2 arquivos \u00b7 5 min] Em `ep07-ato2-adsense.spec.json:174-181` e `ep07-estrutura-hedge.spec.json:124-131` acrescentar `\"value\": 45` / `\"value\": 55` e `\"splitAt\": [in[1], in[1]+1.2]`. A cena hoje \u00e9 2 p\u00edlulas roxas ocas e um borr\u00e3o. Preto sem lift e sem vinheta \u00e9 metade da dist\u00e2ncia entre \"amador\" e a pe\u00e7a aprovada.\n\n5. **Matar o scrim/rule \u00f3rf\u00e3os NO MOTOR (corrige as 7 de uma vez).** [2 linhas \u00b7 1 arquivo \u00b7 5 min] Em `montar-template-v2.mjs:384` e `:386`, condicionar `#scrim` e `#rule` a `RT.hud.hasKick || RT.hud.hasTitle || RT.hud.hasCap || RT.hud.hasEnd` \u2014 os flags J\u00c1 existem em `:250`. Hoje toda cena sem HUD abre com uma cortina preta sobre 64% do quadro (medido: `scrimOp=0.95`, `1100.8px`) e um tra\u00e7o ciano flutuando sobre nada, por ~3,8s \u2014 e a entrada do primeiro personagem acontece debaixo dela. Corrigir no motor \u00e9 1/7 do trabalho de corrigir nas specs, e vacina toda cena futura.\n\n6. **`cutout`: `h` \u2192 `w`.** [6 linhas \u00b7 5 arquivos \u00b7 10 min] `ato1` (caneca, mic, kit), `ato3` (mixer), `hedge` (folha), `fecho` (massa), `macro` (gente) \u2014 todas renderizando 300px.
